# Brief for transcript extraction — Ross Cameron (Warrior Trading) channel sweep, W05-0025

You are reading Ross Cameron YouTube transcripts for a systematic trading project. The project trades US small-cap
momentum stocks with two automated strategies (MCL, MC5) and a live scanner, using 1-minute bars from Interactive
Brokers. We want ONLY things that could change what we code or test. Do not summarise stories or motivation.

## How to read
- Load the tool: ToolSearch query "select:mcp__TubeAlfred__youtube_video_transcript".
- Call youtube_video_transcript with the video id. If the result is saved to a file instead of shown, Read that file
  completely in chunks (offset/limit). Read the WHOLE transcript — record the % actually read.
- Auto-captions garble tickers and numbers; note when a number looks garbled rather than guessing.
- If a transcript is unavailable, record "NO TRANSCRIPT" and move on.

## Already covered — mark anything matching these as COVERED (one line), do not re-explain it
- Five pillars: up >=10%, relative volume >=5x 50-day avg, news catalyst, price $2-$20, float <10M; day volume >=10M
- Trade only the leading % gainer / "obvious" stock; jump to a fresher lower-float name
- Time of day: best 07:00-11:00 ET, sits down at 07:00, stopped trading afternoons; 07:00-09:30 cleanest
- Entries: micro pullback; first 1-min candle to make a new high after a pullback; VWAP break; MACD must be positive
  (no trade when MACD negative); heavier green volume; avoid buying extended far above the 9 EMA
- Exits: half off into strength, rest on first red 1-min close; "breakout or bailout" (exit if no quick move)
- Risk: max daily loss = average daily gain; stop trading after giving back half the day's profit; size up only after
  a cushion; no averaging down; no chasing; 2:1 minimum target ratio
- His stats: ~70% win rate with avg win ~= avg loss (R ~0.8-1.0); 2022 Tradervue 67.3% win, R 0.77, ($103k) early drawdown
- Breaking-news algo spike via micro-pullback 04:00-09:30; buy the dip after an algo flush 09:30-16:00 (no stops
  pre-market so flushes happen after the open); HFT algos withdraw when a stock moves +/-10%
- Level 2 displayed size is unreliable (spoofing)
- Universe from the course deck: up >10%, avg daily volume <10M, price <$20, float <20M, market cap <$500M
- News as a skip-filter was already tested here and did not help

### oxob0x0Xz7s — The Ultimate Day Trading Guide (Full Training Chapters 1 - 10) (3:06:12, stats cited through 2024)
(Chapter-by-chapter working notes; final summary block at end of this video's section.)

#### Ch.1-3 (0:00-0:50): platform, metrics, risk, mindset
1. Rules
 1. Entry needs BOTH MACD positive AND no heavy red-volume selling; if either says no, no trade. - COVERED
 2. Entry = first candle to make a new high vs prior candle's high after pullback, with light selling volume. - COVERED
 3. MACD flat + "high candle" (extended/hot candle) = no trade. - COVERED (extension filter) 
 4. Target ratio 2:1 (risk $100 for $200), breakeven win rate 33% at 2:1. - COVERED
 5. "Aim for ~80% chance of working"; strategies at 60-70% win rate are enough if losses cut quickly. - COVERED (stats)
 6. Two trades per day on small-account challenge days ("each day I only took two trades"), focus not volume. - NEW (~0:09) - a trades-per-day cap of 2 on small-account challenge.
 7. Realistic daily growth in cash account: ~10% on a really good day, typically 3-5% (self-reported). - NEW (~0:23) - daily-return expectation; useful as a sanity cap for backtest daily P&L.
 8. Monthly review loop: sort trades by price, time of day, volume; cut the bucket that loses. Example: in a bad month (March) he lost on stocks above $10, so next month restricted to $2-$10 and made 3.6x more. - NEW (~0:34) - price bucket $2-$10 rather than $2-$20; test price-bucket split.
 9. Biggest winners common denominators (his own Tradervue data over $12M profit): news catalyst, price ~$2/$3-$20 (2-10 "a little better"), already up >=30% on day, RVOL >=5x. Higher RVOL buckets make more money. - PARTLY COVERED (pillars use up>=10%); NEW detail: stock already up 30%+ and "$2-10 a little better than $2-20" (~0:50).
 10. Scanner alert trigger: stock up 10% within 5 minutes while price $2-$20 (audio alert), then he checks news. - NEW (~0:49) - rate-of-change trigger (+10% in 5 min), codeable from 1-min bars.
 11. Scanner alert fields: time, ticker, price, day volume, float, RVOL, gap %, % from previous close. (RVOL 96x example.) - COVERED (scanner idea)
 12. News freshness flame: red = news <2h old, orange = 2-12h, yellow = 12-24h; wants red/orange = early in a breaking-news squeeze. - NEW (~0:58) - needs news timestamps.
 13. Float = only supply variable; low-priced stocks tend to have low floats (6.9M-float biotech example). - COVERED
 14. Brokerage: cash account T+1 settlement, margin needs $25k PDT minimum (US). - not relevant to code.
 15. Big loss -> emotional spiral -> more trades. "Trader rehab": cut size to level that triggers no emotion for min 10 trading days. - COVERED-ish (risk psychology); not codeable.
 16. Beginners use stocks not obeying scanner = losses. Quality setups raise accuracy which lifts profit/loss ratio since poor P/L ratio is caused by a few outlier losers. - NEW framing (~0:44): P/L ratio damage comes from outlier losses -> test losses tail cap.
2. Stats (self-reported)
 - 2017 start $583.15 -> $100k in 45 days -> $335k by Dec 2017; ~$0.5M 2018; >$1M 2019; $10.7M cum by end 2023; $12.3M end 2024 (audited to 2023).
 - Tradervue all-time: avg win ~= avg loss (about $1,300 each, within $18-19), accuracy 66%.
 - Bad month example (March): avg win ~$700 (garbled), avg loss ~$1,100 (garbled "$11,000"), accuracy 61%, net ~$10k; April $36k after limiting to $2-10.
 - First year ~+25-30k, second year -30k (early career).
3. Codeable: #6 trades cap = yes (config); #8 price bucket = codeable from daily/1-min bars; #9 up>=30% = yes; #10 +10%/5min = codeable from 1-min bars; #12 flame = needs news data; #16 = analysis only.

#### Ch.4-8 (0:50-2:05): candles, indicators, micro-pullback, orders, halts
1. Rules
 1. Chart set: 9/20/200 EMA, VWAP, volume bars, MACD; same indicators on daily/5m/1m/10s; MACD used ONLY on 1-min (not 5m/daily/10s). - NEW detail (~1:24) MACD only on 1-min; rest COVERED
 2. MACD cross to negative = end of "front side" of the move; any later breakout attempt "more times than not" rejects; wait it out. Found useful in 2022 bear market for avoiding false breakouts. - COVERED (MACD negative = no trade)
 3. Trade above VWAP = bullish; if price falls below VWAP and holds below, sellers in control, stock not tradeable. - COVERED (VWAP) 
 4. Multi-timeframe check: look at daily for resistance (prior daily highs "look left then up"), then 5-min must be above VWAP with no exit signs, then execute on 1-min. Best trades when 5m and 1m patterns align; he takes non-aligned trades too. - NEW (~1:24-1:26) - 5-min above-VWAP gate and daily resistance levels as targets; codeable from 1-min (resample to 5m) + daily bars.
 5. Support/resistance: VWAP, 9/20/200 EMA act as support on all timeframes; prior resistance becomes support once broken; below those levels they are resistance. - NEW as filter (~1:26) - codeable.
 6. Do not buy the first leg: stop would be the start of the run so 2:1 target unrealistic; wait for pullback ("another train comes"). - COVERED
 7. Micro-pullback definition: spike (scanner hit) -> pullback with first red, second red (confirmed reversal) -> bounce -> buy first candle to make a new high versus previous candle's high. Light selling volume on pullback, MACD open. - COVERED
 8. STOP = low of the pullback ("this could be $100 of risk"); TARGET = retest of high of day (HOD); must check HOD distance gives >=2:1 first. Will sometimes take <2:1 (~1:1) when stock has a big range (example stock $3.40 -> $4.95, price $4.60). - NEW (~1:30-1:33) - stop at pullback low and first target = HOD; codeable from 1-min bars.
 9. Cut losers at max-loss point without hoping; hold winners until an exit indicator, not a price. - COVERED
 10. Exit indicators list (1:34-1:35): (a) high-volume red candle, (b) MACD crossover, (c) topping tail / doji, (d) break of VWAP going down, (e) break of 9 EMA going down; also "two candles making lower and lower" (two steps down), exit on the second candle going red after a topping tail/shooting star. - NEW as a set (~1:34) beyond "first red close": VWAP-break exit, 9EMA-break exit, MACD-cross exit, high-volume-red exit. Codeable from 1-min bars.
 11. Trade only the first and second pullback after the spike; third and fourth pullbacks "a little too risky". - NEW (~1:36) - codeable: count pullbacks since the day's first thrust; test win rate by pullback index.
 12. Gravestone doji / shooting star after strong run = sell signal; do not buy the candle after it. Doji in a sideways range means little. Bottoming tail (hammer) -> buy the second (confirming green) candle, not the hammer itself. - NEW (~1:05-1:12) - codeable (wick/body ratios) but low value; enters after confirmation.
 13. Do not buy after 15 consecutive green candles (extended), wait for pullback. - COVERED (extension filter), gives example number 15 (~1:32).
 14. Trade "obvious" top percentage gainer; best day was number-one leading % gainer in whole market (WKEY, SpaceX news, $98,654 day). - COVERED
 15. Order types: LIMIT orders only (no market orders, no stop orders in premarket); he does not use stop orders because stops are visible to brokers/market makers (stop hunting); buys from the ask / sells to the bid with limit at ask (+ a few cents) to guarantee fill. - NEW (~1:43-1:58) - relevance: execution model (marketable limits, no resting stop). Discretionary/execution.
 16. Hours: 04:00-09:30 premarket, 09:30-16:00 regular, to 20:00 extended; no halts and no stops pre-market; he trades 07:00-10:00 (peak volatility and liquidity); 04:00 start only when travelling in Europe; no afternoon trading ("not profitable for me"). - COVERED
 17. LULD / circuit-breaker halts (regular hours only): bands vs reference price (RP = 5-min average, updated ~every 30s); prior close <$0.75: $0.15; $0.75-$3: 20%; >$3: 10%. Must stay at band edge >=15 s -> 5-min pause. Halts do not exist pre-market, so premarket price action is "cleaner" (no halts). - NEW (~1:50-1:52) - codeable as a halt-risk/gap model: bar gaps after a halt. Needs halt flags/tick data for exactness (IBKR 1-min bars show missing minutes).
 18. Halt-up resumption: during the halt an auction forms; good-news halt typically reopens HIGHER than halt price (WKEY halted $3.70, reopened $4.65, then ran to $6.40; he bought $4.97 after resume). Resumption quote (LULD bid/ask) visible only on better platforms. - NEW (~1:53-1:55) - needs tick/halt data; observation: upside halts reopen higher on strong news (gap up on resumption), halted names are a trigger to look. 
 19. Halt scanner alerts him to stocks that "something just happened" in. - NEW (~1:53) - code: use missing-bar/gap as trigger.
 20. Level 2 use: read distance between asks (thin ask: e.g. 9.03, 9.04, 9.11, 9.17, 9.20, 9.47 means stock can jump to 9.47 on 10k shares), big seller/buyer visible. Spread example 12 cents on a $9 stock. - NEW (~2:04) thin-ask idea - needs level 2 (note: spoofing caveat already COVERED).
 21. Time and sales (tape) green=lifting offers, used for tape reading. - needs tape (continues in next chunk).
2. Stats (self-reported)
 - WKEY day: +$98,654 (another figure $116k also quoted "finished up about $116,000"). Spread 12c on $9 stock. Halt reference band percentages above.
3. Codeable: #1 yes (config), #4 yes, #5 yes, #8 yes, #10 yes (five exit rules), #11 yes, #17/#18/#19 partly (halts need tick/halt data), #20 needs level 2.

#### Ch.8-9 (2:05-2:35): order entry, risk, trading plan, sizing
1. Rules
 1. Tape reading: eye on the ask; buys AFTER seeing a big buy print on time and sales ("wait for someone else to jump"; prefers not to be first). - NEW (~2:08) - needs level 2 / tape.
 2. Level 2 veto: if a 250,000-share sell order sits on the ask above entry he skips even a perfect chart pattern ("that chart will never do what I thought"). Thin ask (big gaps between ask levels) = fast mover. - NEW (~2:06) - needs level 2 (spoofing caveat already COVERED).
 3. Exit indicator #6: big seller on level 2 or burst of red on time and sales (in addition to the five chart exits above). - NEW (~2:31) - needs level 2 / tape.
 4. Scale-in: starter, then half, then full size by repeated buy clicks (2-3 clicks); but sells in ONE hotkey: sell quarter / half / full (Ctrl Z/X/C). Hotkey buy = limit at ask + $0.10 offset when stock moving nicely. - NEW (~2:08-2:12) - order-style detail: limit at ask+10c; codeable as a fill assumption (ask + 10c cap).
 5. Risk per trade is entry minus stop (not capital deployed); stop = low of the pullback; entry = first candle to make new high; target = HOD; 2:1 minimum. - COVERED (see Ch.4-8)
 6. Position sizing in the day: FIRST trade of the day at quarter or half size (typically 15-20 cents/share risk or reward); if it wins (cushion) trade 2 at full size; if it loses, stay at quarter/half; once at daily goal, scale down to protect; if back below the cushion, go back to quarter size. - NEW (~2:24-2:27) as precise scheme; "size up only after a cushion" is COVERED. Codeable: size multiplier by day P&L state.
 7. "Three strikes": 3 losers in a row (or three losses of X amount) = done for the day. - NEW (~2:26, 2:30) - codeable consecutive-loss stop. (Max daily loss COVERED.)
 8. If he cannot reach the daily goal within the first few trades, the day is "difficult" and he keeps size small (usually he hits the goal within the first few trades). - NEW (~2:28) - codeable "early trades expectancy gate".
 9. Abandon a stock completely when it fails to keep going up; wait for the next one. - COVERED (jump to fresher name)
 10. Beginner sample plan numbers: price $1-$10, float <10M, up 25% with news, RVOL 10x, stock must be in TOP THREE leading % gainers, 08:00-10:00 sweet spot, first pullback of bull flag with MACD positive. Sample daily goal = max loss = $25 on 100 shares. - NEW numbers (~2:24): price $1-$10 (vs $2-$20), up 25%, RVOL 10x, top-3 rank. Top-3 rank codeable only with a market-wide scanner snapshot; the rest need float/news.
 11. Phase 2 proof: one trade per day for 10 days, require >=5 of 10 green days (6-7 ideal), accuracy >=60%, positive net, avg loss smaller than avg win. - NEW (~2:32-2:34) - validation criterion, not a trading rule.
 12. Limit orders only; turns off order confirmations for speed; sticky-note hotkeys. - not relevant.
2. Stats (self-reported)
 - Typical first-trade move ~15-20 cents/share (winner or loser) at quarter size.
 - His expected loss streak cap = 3 trades; daily goal reached "usually within first few trades".
3. Codeable: #4 yes (fill model), #6 yes, #7 yes, #8 yes, #1/#2/#3 need level 2/tape, #10 needs float+news except price/up%/rank, #11 analysis only.

#### Ch.9-10 (2:35-3:06): scaling, drawdown sizing, hot/cold market, taxes, discipline, final quiz
1. Rules
 1. Scale-up ladder: after each 10 clean days at a size, add share size (100 -> 150 -> 200 ...); keep one trade/day cap first, then 2/day, then 3/day, each for weeks/months, and only after a drawdown-and-recovery at that level. - NEW (~2:36-2:38) - process rule, not strategy; discretionary.
 2. After a big loss: cut size to ONE QUARTER of full size and keep it there until ~50% of the loss is recouped, then return to full size. - NEW (~2:39) - codeable drawdown-based size multiplier (0.25x until 50% of drawdown recovered).
 3. Hot vs cold market: "ease off the throttle when cold, pedal to the metal when hot"; size up when hot (pushes risk tolerance), rein in when cold; cold market = losses mount, recouped only when heat returns; he judges it from own results and trader-community chatter. No numeric definition. - NEW (~2:40-2:41) - discretionary (no numbers); proxies would be recent P&L and count of qualifying movers.
 4. Quiz rule: heavy-volume selling (high-volume red candles) on the pullback = NO even if MACD positive; MACD negative = NO even if volume fine; both must say yes. - COVERED
 5. Marginal setup (a couple of topping tails, one slightly higher-volume candle but MACD open): take with SMALLER size; "if it's not a hard yes, it's a no" for beginners. - NEW (~3:03) - size-by-setup-quality; discretionary (needs scoring).
 6. "Hard yes" examples: $5 to $7 in two candles with open MACD and strong volume profile; $10 to $14 on number-one leading gainer with MACD open but coming down is still a yes. - NEW (~3:03-3:04) - weak; suggests big, fast thrust with positive MACD = high conviction. Codeable as thrust size (e.g. +40% in two bars) filter for test.
 7. Broker remark: Interactive Brokers "is not great for people that like to trade stocks under $5" (reason not stated; likely cost/route). Lightspeed (his own broker) allows choosing routing destination; IBKR/thinkorswim route for payment-for-order-flow. - NEW (~2:48) - relevant to cost model: check IBKR per-share commission minimum and fill quality on sub-$5 names; (his claim, unquantified).
 8. Taxes (Roth IRA, mark-to-market, Puerto Rico, wash sales, 475(f)): no trading rules. - not relevant.
 9. Discipline guard rails: keep size within comfort, strict loss guidelines, journal and metrics, list emotional triggers and stop trading when they occur. - COVERED-ish; not codeable.
2. Stats (self-reported)
 - Roth IRA: $188k contributions (3 x $6k) grown to >$7M trading profits (self-reported, unverified). Account figures: $12.3M verified profits to 2024.
 - Quoted beginner target: 60% accuracy / >=5 of 10 green days in a one-trade-a-day proof phase.
3. Codeable: #2 yes (sizing state machine), #6 yes (thrust filter), #7 needs IBKR cost check, #1/#3/#5 discretionary.
4. Single most useful NEW thing: stop = low of the pullback and first target = HOD retest with a check that HOD distance >= 2x risk, taking only the first two pullbacks after the spike (1:30-1:36); alongside the five-way exit set (VWAP break, 9 EMA break, MACD cross, high-volume red, topping tail) beyond "first red close", and the day-sizing state machine (quarter/half first trade, 3 losers in a row = done, 0.25x until 50% of a loss is recouped).
   Honest note: this is a beginner course; most numbers are restatements of the five pillars/micro-pullback already covered. Genuinely new numeric items: first-two-pullbacks-only, stop at pullback low/target HOD, VWAP/9EMA/MACD-cross exit set, price $1-10 / up 25% / RVOL 10x / top-3 gainer in sample plan, 3-losers-in-a-row stop, 0.25x size after big loss until 50% recouped, LULD band numbers and halt-resumption gap-up behaviour, MACD only on 1-min plus 5-min above-VWAP gate, +10% in 5 min scanner trigger, news freshness <2h/2-12h/12-24h.
5. Portion read: 100% (all 186 minute-blocks of the 3:06:12 transcript read; auto-captions garble some figures, e.g. Roth contribution limit and a March avg-loser "$11,000").

### B81TMhUpz50 — 27 Years of Trading Knowledge in 3hrs and 5mins (3:05:03, year ~2024 — "this past September", 2024 YTD ~$800k)
[IN-PROGRESS NOTES, part 1: 00:00-1:03:00 read; final consolidated layout follows at the end of this entry]
- NEW (33:35) Start-of-day sizing: max 1/4 of full size (example full=1000 sh -> 250 sh) until the day is up +$0.20/share (x quarter size = $50 in his example); only then full size. If first 3 trades are losers, stay small/stop. Self-reported: introduced June (2024), then ~45 consecutive green days; Jul $232k, Aug $171k. Codeable (size ladder by day P&L) - needs only fills/P&L.
- NEW (15:00-17:30) Market regime by count of A-quality setups/day: hot 15-25, medium 10-15, cold 0-10 (B-quality: hot 40-75). Accuracy by tier (self-reported): A ~70%, B ~60%, C ~50%, lower 30-50%. On colder days even A setups ~65%. Codeable only as a scanner-hit-count regime proxy.
- NEW stat (31:30): winning days avg +$10,000, accuracy 71%, avg win $1,300, avg loss $1,000; losing days avg -$8,000, accuracy only 50% (deviation to lower-quality setups). $16M total on winning days. Self-reported.
- NEW stat (55:58-58:00): stocks <$2 produced <4% (~$400k) of ~$13M lifetime profit; sub-$1 stocks trade in 1/100th-cent ticks, slow until they break $1, big sellers sit on ask. $2-$20 sweet spot (COVERED); small-account challenge drops cap to <$10 (NEW, minor).
- NEW stat (59:00): 99% of profit from stocks up >=10% at entry (COVERED by pillar). Stats quoted: 20-trading-day month, needs ~100 trades at ~$1,000/trade avg, 70% accuracy.
[part 2: read to 1:47:53]
- NEW (1:06:09) Day-of-week: companies release good news early in week (Mon-Wed), slows toward Fri; Friday afternoon scanner hit with no catalyst = skip. Codeable from daily bars (dow bucket of P&L) for a test.
- NEW (1:11-1:14) Bonus criteria (not required): recent IPO; recent reverse split (float collapses, e.g. 20:1 split turns 10M sh into 500k); all-time highs ("blue sky", no overhead); prior-day volume <100,000 sh (so RVOL explodes). Codeable: ATH / prior-day vol <100k from daily bars; IPO/reverse-split/float need float or corporate-action data.
- COVERED (1:07, 1:08, 1:14) float <20M, RVOL >=5, biggest winners from top 3-4 % gainers; scanners cast looser filters then manual filter.
- NEW (1:19-1:33) Fundamental check only when stock is already extended (e.g. +50%): read balance sheet cash, net loss, shelf registration (S-3; e.g. $100M shelf vs 23M float = dilution risk), Nasdaq delisting notice -> risk of crash/offering. He says he does NOT check filings on most trades; "you could get away with doing zero fundamental analysis". Needs filing/news data; discretionary.
- NEW (1:32) "When it rains it pours": when one small cap is hot dozens are hot; when cold, cold across the board (regime clustering). Same-day: gainers count as regime proxy.
- NEW (1:44-1:46) Entry detail: wait for a candle with a bottoming tail during the pullback, then buy the next candle as it breaks that candle's high (confirm with green orders on L2); entry at the break not on close. Stop = low of the pullback (example entry 7.30, low 7.10 = 20c risk). Target >= 2x risk (40c); SKIP trade if room to next resistance/profit potential < 2x risk (example 5c potential vs 20c risk = no trade). Stop placement NEW; the rest COVERED. Codeable from 1-min bars (pullback low stop, 2R room filter vs prior high/HOD).
- NEW (1:43) Buy only the FIRST pullback after the initial spike; if pullback keeps making red candles all the way down, no trade. Quality of stock (5 pillars) overrides pattern. Codeable (first-pullback flag).
- Stat: his profit by price: $2-5, 5-10, 10-20 all contribute (chart not quantified in transcript).
[part 3: read to 2:27:00]
- COVERED (1:48) first candle to make new high after pullback = entry; max loss = low of pullback; target = high of day. Worked example: entry 4.24, stop 4.18 (6c risk), target ~4.50 (25c, "4:1"). Stock quality (5 pillars) outranks pattern.
- NEW (1:52-1:56, 2:00) Buy the FIRST and SECOND pullback only; after the 2nd he gets "more cautious / doesn't chase". Pullback #3+ = no. Example: 2nd pullback entry 7.73-ish retest, ran to 8.37+ (+104% on day). Codeable: count pullbacks since the day's first spike on 1-min bars. Also: may ADD back at same price after a bail-out if first-new-high then triggers (he bailed break-even on an early/anticipatory entry, re-entered at the same price when candle made new high and it ran). Early entry before the new-high candle = missed more often (self-described).
- NEW (1:54, 2:18) Uses 10-second chart for earlier trigger (6 candles/min); trigger = bottoming-tail candle then 10s candle goes green. Codeable only with sub-minute bars (IBKR 5s bars available).
- NEW (2:04-2:06, 2:09) Volume-profile filter: want high volume on green/buy candles and LIGHT volume on red pullback candles; failed example = pop on light volume followed immediately by heavy-volume selling -> no entry even with news (floats 34M and 1.45M both failed). Codeable from 1-min bars: pullback-candle volume < advance-candle volume.
- COVERED (2:09-2:11) MACD: best trades on first leg while 9/20 EMAs diverge ("MACD open") before convergence/crossover; negative MACD = no trade; consolidation after convergence is choppy. COVERED for MACD >0; the "front side only / before MACD cross" framing is a mild extension.
- NEW (2:01-2:02) Chart set: 9 EMA, 20 EMA, 200 EMA (1m and 5m), VWAP, volume pane, MACD. "Primarily trade when stock is ABOVE these MAs" (above 9/20/200 EMA and above VWAP; below VWAP = resistance for rest of day; dip off VWAP = buy opportunity). VWAP break COVERED; the 200 EMA above-filter is NEW and codeable (1-min 200 EMA needs 200 prior bars incl. premarket).
- NEW (2:07-2:19) Half-dollar / whole-dollar levels: clusters of resistance at x.50 and x.00 (people place profit targets there). First pullback often forms at the first half/whole dollar; breaking through then dipping = retest; he takes partial profit as price breaks through a half/whole dollar (e.g. 2.75 entry -> sell some at 3.10; 7.31 entry -> sold at 8.23-8.26 after breaking 8.00); full exit if it straddles/fails to hold the level. Codeable from 1-min bars: partial exit at next x.00/x.50 level; entry filter on distance to next .50/.00 (the 2:1 room test).
- COVERED (2:15-2:16) Exit watch: want price to go up within ~60 seconds, no red candle/red tape, no big sellers (example: 50,000-sh sell order "concerning"; 200 and 2,000 sh not). "Hold as long as I can if no exit signal". Needs L2 for sell-order size.
- NEW (2:23-2:26) Go-live gate (self-reported process): 10 days of 1-trade/day in sim, require net profit > 0, accuracy >60% (target 70%+), profit/loss ratio > 1:1; then 10 days live at tiny size. Not a trading-rule for the bot but a promotion criterion template for paper->live (accuracy >60%, PL ratio >1, 10 days).
- Quoted position: 2,500 shares on an $8 stock (~$20k) on a "full position" in a quality setup.
- NEW (2:26-2:27) Slippage warning: expect to exit 15c worse than the quote on a fast drop (e.g. $3.00 stock sells at 2.85, not 2.99, = $150 on 1,000 sh). Relevant to cost modelling.

(a) max loss COVERED in principle; (b) NEW. Codeable (consecutive-loser counter; losers must be "sizable", threshold not stated).

- NEW (2:54-2:56) Scaling order: raise share size first (one trade/day, target 10-20c/share/day, up to 2-3k shares), add trade count second; cash account naturally caps trades/day. Beginner sim target: 20c/share/day.
- NEW (2:29, 2:46-2:50) Metrics reviews (self-reported): in Sept 2024 afternoon trades (after ~2pm, into the close) cut accuracy, PL ratio and consistency; "if stopped at 11:00 a.m. every day would have been better off" (COVERED time-of-day). Aug 2024 power-hour trades had worked, Sept did not = unstable. A loss late in the day after a long green day is emotionally bigger -> spiral.

Also in a later 30-day span losses came from higher-price stocks ("did not get paid"). Suggests $2-$10 beats $10-$20 for him. ### W6BnX1T3KZ0 — How to Day Trade: The Plain Truth, audiobook (4:31:21, book published c.2023-24; stats up to 2022)
Transcript: auto-captions, 7349 segments, 280k chars, read 100% in 8 chunks. Timestamps below are h:mm:ss / mm:ss in the video. The book is ~80% mindset/memoir/basics; the codeable content is concentrated in ch.2 (40:00-1:20:00), ch.6-8 (2:09:00-3:30:00) and ch.10 (3:45:00-4:03:00).

#### Ch.1-2 (0:00-1:20:00) — memoir, his own filter derivation
1. Rules/numbers
 - COVERED: five common denominators (up >10%, high rel vol, news, price $2-20, low float); trades the gap scanner / top gappers; waits for the stock to prove it is moving; "get green and shut it down"; 2-hour morning window; no averaging down, no revenge trade, no trading past max daily loss.
 - NEW (46:30) Sub-$1 stocks: "consistently losing money on stocks under $1" in his own trade-history study; he rarely trades them (re-stated 2:14:08). Our price floor $2 is consistent; he never tested $1-2 separately.
 - NEW (41:00-42:00) Price sweet spot from his own history: "focusing primarily on $2-$10, certainly not more than $20". Losses > $20 were outsized (wider ranges, loss exceeded planned max). Pillar says $2-20; his own data says $2-10 is the core. Testable: P&L split $2-10 vs $10-20.
 - NEW (1:18:54) Scanner "Guard rail #1" as printed in the book: up >10%, REL VOL ONLY >= 2 (not 5), price $2-10 (not more than 20), news today, float <50M "and preferably <20M". Differs from our pillars (RVOL 5x, float <10M). Looser floor than the pillars; the book's own numbers.
 - NEW (46:52) Float used to be his <50M cut; market cap implied max ~$1B (20 x 50M, 2:11:00).
 - NEW (50:05) "Trade even after three consecutive losers = a red flag that should have told me to stop." Later (3:00:30): "if I take three or four trades in a row and lose on all of them, shut it down." A consecutive-loss stop (3-4) is not in the covered list.
 - NEW (1:04:19-1:07:10) His own automated scanner/bot: criteria = % change on day, volume rel to average, price band $2-20, total volume, rate of change, volume in last 5 min, cents moved in last 5 min, spread; order = N shares with a 15-cent trailing stop. Backtest over 30 days with 1,000-share orders = +$20,000 (self-reported), but live results failed: (a) slippage on entries and exits not modelled, (b) market "jumped over" buy orders so the best trades often did not fill. Net: removing the unfilled best trades turned success into failure. Direct warning for our backtests: apply adverse slippage and drop/penalise fills on the biggest-gap bars.
2. Stats (self-reported): accuracy ~69% (11:35); audited >$10M profit; $583.15 account from 2017-01-01 to >$100k in 45 days, >$1M in 2019, >$10M in 2022 (1:15:00); first-year gain ~$30k then given back in year 2.
3. Codeable: price band split = daily/1-min bars; RVOL>=2 vs 5 = daily bars; float <50M vs <10M = needs float data; 3-consecutive-loss stop = codeable from own trade log; slippage/missed-fill modelling = codeable from 1-min bars (assume fill at next bar high/open+X, drop gap-through bars).

#### Ch.3-5 (1:20:00-2:08:00) — myths, gear
1. COVERED: 7:30-11:30 ET is most of the volume (1:42:12; pre-market news reaction); top-3 percentage gainers across 3 screens (watch 2, trade 1) (1:51:33).
 - NEW (1:51:33) Screen layout implies selection set = top 3 % gainers only (trade the leader, watch #2 and #3). Already approximately covered by "trade leading gainer"; the explicit watchlist size of 3 is new but minor.

#### Ch.6 (2:09:00-2:30:00) — market basics, spread, halts
1. Rules
 - NEW (2:16:22-2:17:00) Algos switch off once a stock is up 10% on the day and certainly after 5-10% in 10-15 minutes; spreads may widen "10 to 15% on a stock under $20 and that is perfectly okay with me". Sweet spot: not a 1-cent spread, not a 50-cent spread. "More than anything I look for fast rate of change." (Rate-of-change trigger: +5-10% in 10-15 min.) Partly covered by "HFT withdraws at +/-10%"; the 5-10%-in-10-15-min rate-of-change figure and the spread tolerance are new.
 - NEW (2:17:00) Relative volume has a sweet spot: huge RVOL on a stock with tiny average volume (his example: avg 100 sh, 10,000 today = RVOL 100) can be untradeable for lack of liquidity. Needs a total-volume floor next to RVOL (we already have day vol >=10M).
 - NEW (2:09:49-2:11:00) Market-cap bands he uses: large >$10B, mid $2-10B, small $300M-2B, micro <$300M, nano <$50M. Trades large caps only on IPO day or a once-a-decade news event.
 - NEW (2:22:50) Margin: not until 6-12 months of profitability and a recovered drawdown. Shorting: not until 3-6 months of sim profit. (Process rules, not strategy.)
 - NEW (2:25:06) Shorts: a stock went $20 -> >$2,500 in under a week; short risk is unbounded. No short setup is taught in the book at all (only long momentum).
2. Stats: none.

#### Ch.7-8 (2:30:00-3:30:00) — risk
1. Rules
 - COVERED: 2:1 target minimum (2:44:33; "aim a little high, but keep at least 1:1 overall"); max daily loss = daily profit target (3:00:00; example $200/$200); no averaging down; no moving the stop mid-trade; no chasing.
 - NEW (2:43:59) Break-even accuracy table: 1:1 needs 50%, 1:2 inverted needs 66%, 2:1 needs 33%. He sets 25% of daily target as per-trade max loss => max daily loss = ~4 full losers.
 - NEW (3:00:24) Per-trade max loss ~= 25% of the daily profit target; so daily target = 4R. Stop for the day after 3-4 consecutive full losers.
 - NEW (2:52:23) Stop placement is "a recent level of support or resistance... a boundary that invalidates the trade", not arbitrary or % based (no number given).
 - NEW (3:06:49) Guardrail 10: no single trade carries so much risk that it can erase more than 1-2 previous winners; adjust risk in SMALL increments inside a session (example: $100 max loss -> $150 up or $75 down); no drastic mid-session change. Codeable as a per-trade risk cap relative to day's realised P&L.
 - NEW (2:53:29) After breaking news "wait for the dust to settle" before trading; extreme volatility makes loss larger than planned (conflicts mildly with the covered micro-pullback-after-algo-spike; the book's wording is on wide-range candles).
 - NEW (2:55:06) Volatility halt: >10% move inside a rolling 5 minutes => 5-minute halt (LULD). Halts reopen: halted up usually reopens higher, halted down lower; T12 halts (weeks/months) never reopened higher in his experience; penny stocks +500% on no news are T12 candidates. Rule: limit size on stocks with no news / unconfirmed rumour. Codeable only with halt feed; 1-min bars show a gap/missing bars.
 - NEW (3:01:05-3:01:34) He uses a broker-side max position size (shares and dollars) and max daily loss as a hard backstop. Already standard for us (kill switch).
 - NEW (3:22:28) Giving back more than half of the day's profit after hitting the goal "can itself trigger emotional hijack". (Covered as the half-giveback stop, but this is the first instance of the stat that hitting the goal then giving back is the trigger.)
 - NEW (3:15:15) Throttle: ~top 10 days a year exceed the daily goal by >= 4x each and give ~20% of the year's profit (so ~250 days, 10 = 20%). Hence he does NOT stop at the daily goal on hot days (but beginners do: "get green and shut it down").
 - NEW (3:16:09) Best days cluster in time (market hot), worst days cluster (overtrading in cold market); hot/cold can flip day to day in choppy markets. Rule: "trade the market I'm in" (guardrail 11, 3:16:33). No numeric hot/cold definition; the only observable he cites is "whether stocks keep hitting or failing to hit my profit targets".
 - NEW (3:20:05-3:21:00) Hot market: hold the full position (or sell only a quarter) at the first target, wait for a clear exit signal; cold market: sell all or half at the first target, sell sooner as stocks keep failing targets. Exit rule is state-dependent on a rolling "targets hit vs failed" count. Codeable as: first-target fraction sold = f(share of last N trades that reached target).
 - NEW (3:18:11) Share-size ramp: raise upper size limit ~1,000 shares/week (10,000 -> 15,000 = 10% of old limit per week), step back down to base in a drawdown; never 10k -> 20k overnight.
 - NEW (3:26:17-3:28:26) "Trader rehab" after a drawdown: max share size cut, max daily loss cut to 1/2 or 1/4 of normal, ONE trade per day for 5 days aiming for 5 small green days; keep tight rails until ~half the drawdown is recouped (to avoid "stair-stepping" down). Codeable as a drawdown-state machine for sizing/daily-loss.
 - NEW (3:39:42) Long-body green candle: be in it, but do not buy at the top; wait for pullback (covered as "avoid extended", no number).
2. Stats (self-reported): ~"tens of thousands of trades"; 90% accuracy with one uncapped loser = net red (illustrative, not data); even good days contain losers; a 40% win rate day can be green.
3. Codeable: break-even maths, 25%-of-target per-trade risk, 3-4 consecutive-loss stop, per-trade risk cap vs day's P&L, drawdown state machine, hot/cold exit scaling = all codeable from our own trade log + 1-min bars. Support/resistance stop = discretionary unless defined (e.g. pullback low). Halts = needs halt data.

#### Ch.9 (3:30:00-3:44:00) — candlesticks
1. Rules
 - COVERED: pullback/new-high entries, extended entries, MACD/VWAP/EMA mention only.
 - NEW (3:36:00) Doji after 5-10 consecutive green candles = potential reversal (no stat). Topping tail (long upper wick, close near open after 3-4 big greens) is one of his "favourite indicators" to EXIT a winner (3:36:53) and to look for reversal shorts. Hammer after a downtrend is confirmed only when next candle breaks the hammer high (3:37:56); shooting star confirmed if next candle goes lower. Codeable from 1-min OHLC (wick/body ratio, close position in range) but with no stated thresholds.

#### Ch.10 (3:45:00-4:03:00) — finding the right stocks
1. Rules
 - COVERED: leading % gainer, "is it the obvious one" (4:02:03: if you have to talk yourself into it, skip), biotech/FDA catalysts, news catalyst.
 - NEW (3:45:04) Guardrail 12: the right stock matters more than the right pattern; a good pattern on the wrong stock loses.
 - NEW (3:46:56) He wants stocks "already up 15 to 20% or more since yesterday" (pillar says >=10%).

(Contradictory statement at 3:46:56: "may buy again, even with a somewhat higher position".) Codeable: per-symbol re-entry counter, decreasing size, stop after first loser.

When the #1 gainer is a stock he will not trade, the other names are "in its shadow" and underperform in volatility and RVOL (3:50:04). Needs news text for buyout detection; the "shadow" effect is testable: P&L of #2/#3 on days when #1 was excluded by price/float.

 - NEW (3:54:21) His big winners cluster together and so do his losers (regime persistence; same as ch.8).

 - NEW (4:00:29) "Former runner" status (strong stock component 6): scanner for stocks that ran on a prior day; he sets a LOWER total-volume filter for them so he sees the move early, but still requires high RVOL today, else skip even if it is a former runner (4:01:30). Former runners are sometimes a distraction vs the day's truly obvious stock. Codeable: prior-day-runner flag from daily bars (threshold not stated).
 - NEW (3:52:00-3:54:00) Fear/greed carry-over: "greed = one-day-old FOMO"; a stock that had a huge run lifts the next day's top gainers (coattail). Day-2 effect; no numbers.

### w2owyBNunDQ — The Moving Average Trading Strategy You Need! (Full Training) (59:58, year not stated; a single trading day with +$222k P&L; example stock CR, "Chevy Corvette wheels")
1. Rules
 1. Chart set: only 9 EMA, 20 EMA, 200 EMA (no 50, no 100); MACD standard 12/26/9 (default settings, deliberately uncustomised). Chosen 9 EMA over 10 because it signals a hair earlier. — NEW (~06:50, ~09:30, ~23:30)
 2. 9/20 EMA cross as a trade/no-trade switch: when 9 EMA crosses BELOW 20 EMA stop buying that stock and walk away; when 9 crosses back ABOVE 20, "game time", trading allowed again. Only trade while 9 > 20. — NEW (~21:50). (Partly overlaps COVERED "MACD must be positive", but a separate rule: 9>20 gate.)
 3. MACD signal-line cross occurs slightly BEFORE the 9/20 cross; he says MACD is the best/fastest of three trend-shift tools (MACD cross > 9/20 cross > Alligator 13/8/5). — NEW (~24:00-25:30)
 4. Alligator (13/8/5 EMAs; trade only when 5>8>13 "open"; do not trade when crossed/sleeping). He does not use it himself (clutter, lags). — NEW but explicitly not used (~10:00-16:00)
 5. "Moving average pullback" (MA pullback) = third entry type after micro pullback and bull flag: after a breakout, price consolidates sideways/gently down to rest on the 20 EMA (or 9 EMA just above it), holds it, then pulls away on volume; enter near the MA (tight stop just under support) and/or on break of descending resistance (wedge). Used when stock has stopped going straight up and a micro pullback is no longer available. — NEW (~17:30-20:30, ~34:00-37:00, ~48:00)
 6. MA pullback is NOT used on a stock moving straight up (MAs far away): use micro pullback / bull flag there. — NEW (~16:30-17:30, ~33:00)
 7. Wants volume before entering: waited for the green candle that carried volume, not the earlier ones; first candle to make new high after a high-volume red candle rejected (not a clean flag; too much selling volume) — skipped. — COVERED (heavier green volume) (~18:00-20:00)
 8. Stop on a 5-min-chart-based entry = low of the last 5-minute candle (example: entry ~5.15-5.20, stop ~5.00, 15-20 cent stop). Stop on the MA-pullback example = just below the low of the pullback / support, ~10 cents away. — NEW (~38:30, ~48:00). Note the 5-min-low stop is a specific placement rule.
 9. Multi-timeframe alignment: 5-min shows bull flag / clean breakout while 1-min shows only sideways consolidation near MAs; a 5-min breakout is "more powerful" than a 1-min one; check 1-min for warning signs (big red volume bar with topping tail) that 5-min hides. — NEW (~39:30-41:00)
 10. Stock consolidating in the top 15% of its day range = strong bullish sentiment (shorts had chance and failed). — NEW (~40:50)
 11. Daily-chart check before trading: look for gap (no overhead resistance until the gap low), distance to 200 EMA on daily (if 200 EMA is just overhead, worry it is resistance; here it was far above, ~$27 vs ~$8.5 price), recent reverse split (lowers float). Example stock: +425%, 14M volume, 600k float, $8.52. — NEW (~30:30-32:45) (daily-chart overhead resistance / 200 EMA distance); reverse split = float flag

 13. No-news stock up 50-100%+: in a hot market will still consider it; in a cold market more cautious (shorts will fade it). — COVERED-ish (news skip-filter tested, did not help); regime-conditional wording is NEW (~29:00-30:00)
 14. Level-2 / psychological levels: loves trading near half-dollar and whole-dollar levels; entry as close as possible to a whole dollar that has been held repeatedly ("triple support": whole dollar + ascending support line + MA). — NEW (~45:00-48:30, ~50:00-51:30)
 15. Trendlines: draw descending resistance (wedge) and ascending support through the most touches; break of descending resistance = entry trigger; breaks of wedge usually come before the apex; a break then quick retest is normal, "can it hold above". — NEW (~38:00, ~52:30-55:00)
 16. Scaling: constantly adds and takes partial profit ("add add add take a little profit"), trades the range in waves, 7+ trades on one squeeze; adds on dips and on break of high of day when shorts expected to cover (level-2 sell walls ~25-30k shares trigger partial exits). — NEW as style (~41:30-43:00, ~46:00-47:30); sizing numbers not given. Level-2 dependence = needs level 2 / tape.

 18. Cons of MA pullback: by the time price is at MA, MACD has crossed and bull flag has failed, so it is NOT the first trade of the day (easier trades are earlier); more ambiguous entry; longer to form; risk of "jack-knife" false breakout (pop then instant reversal), more common in cold markets. — NEW (~51:30-57:00)

Market regime: hot market = trade more frequently and take B and even C quality setups, MA pullback and long consolidations OK; cold market (e.g. 30% market correction / bear) = only A setups per the stock-selection worksheet, micro pullbacks on the front side, bull flags, maybe ABCD; NO long-consolidation MA pullbacks, probably no red-to-green, no head-and-shoulders; still use MACD and MA cross as on/off. — NEW (~58:00-59:30)

### js25lIZMUSY — The Simplest Day Trading Strategy for Beginners (with ZERO experience) (1:54:59, year ~2024; "2017 account" through Dec 2023 quoted)
1. Rules
 1. Five pillars (up >=10%, RVOL >=5x, news, low price, float <10M) incl. "~100 of ~10,000 stocks are up >10% on a given day; 95 of top-100 gainers are under $30"; top-10 gainers' floats mostly <10M. — COVERED (~19:00-22:00)
 2. Price sweet spot from his own Tradervue data: profitable mostly $2-$20, most profit made $5-$10; he would tell a newcomer $1/$2-$20, tighter $5-$10. March (a weak month, ~+$20k) he lost money on stocks >$10 and made it on $2-$10, so for April he traded only $2-$10, then widened again when market improved. — NEW (the $5-$10 sweet-spot band and the "price >$10 loses" finding) (~16:30-17:15, ~77:45-79:00). $2-$20 itself COVERED.
 3. Float >40-50M: ignores entirely (does not even check news or chart); "backfired sometimes but more often right". — COVERED (float <10M)
 4. Supply/demand imbalance as a ratio: day volume vs float (e.g. 58M volume on 930k float = ~62x turnover; 75M on 4M float, +200%; halve the float and % move could double). Larger volume/float imbalance = larger % move. — NEW as a ranking feature (~14:30-23:30). Needs float.
 5. Multiplying factors: headline contains the current hot-theme keyword (AI, Bitcoin, Covid, drones...), hot sector, recent IPO, recent SPAC, recent reverse split -> exponentially larger % gains for same headline; be wary of "keyword stuffing" headlines (e.g. unrelated CEO letter congratulating Nvidia; spiked +100% then faded). — NEW (~27:00-30:00). (Reverse-split also appears in the sibling video.)
 6. Catalyst ranking: clinical-trial results (surprise) > FDA approval (already priced by trial) ; earnings can move low-price companies; new contracts. — NEW but qualitative (~24:00-27:00). Needs news data. (News skip-filter COVERED as tested.)
 7. Daily-chart context before entry: ATH/all-time-low context, nearby support/resistance "look left and up", 200 EMA on daily (stock far below 200 EMA = fine, nearby 200 EMA = resistance). Old resistance candles on tiny volume (e.g. ~70k shares vs 120M today) are irrelevant. — NEW (~41:30-45:00). Codeable from daily bars (distance to 200 EMA, overhead prior highs); volume-weighted relevance of old highs is codeable.
 8. Half-dollar / whole-dollar levels act as resistance and support: stock pushes up, stalls at level, breaks, goes to next half/whole; after a parabolic move through 2-3 levels expect a steep pullback that finds support at a half/whole level; "trade the first and second pullbacks at the beginning of the move", not the extended parabolic top. Longer it holds just under the level without rolling over, better odds of break. Buys "just before the apex" of the breakout. — NEW (~45:00-50:30). Codeable from 1-min bars (distance to nearest .00/.50, count of failed tests of level).
 9. Micro pullback detail: buy as it curls back up only after seeing other buyers (tape confirmation); stop = low of the pullback; accepts a couple of small losses and re-enters on next attempt (2nd/3rd try). — COVERED (micro pullback); "wait for tape buyers" = needs level 2 / tape.
 10. "More important to trade the right stock than the right pattern": imperfect pattern on the leader is fine. — COVERED
 11. Level 2 as final filter: large seller on offer (e.g. 1,000,000 sh at 7.50) = do not buy the breakout; enter just before a large offer is eaten down ("countdown" 50k->40k->30k on time & sales); large bid = sentiment. — NEW (~67:00-70:30). Needs level 2 / tape. (COVERED: displayed size unreliable/spoofing, so treat as caveat.)
 12. Time frames used: 5-min, 1-min, daily, 10-sec; executes on 1-min and 10-sec; entry "break of 7.45-7.50" on 1-min. — NEW minor (~40:30-42:00). 10-second bars not available in our feed (1-min bars).
 13. Trading hours: began at 9:30, then (pandemic) 9:28, 9:20, 9:15 as pre-market volume came; volume heavy 07:00 to ~10-11:00, light 04:00-07:00 because commission-free brokers don't allow pre-7:00; 07:00 is "the new open". — COVERED
 14. Risk/reward: aims 2:1 but "usually closes closer to 1:1"; break-even accuracy 50% at 1:1 and 33% at 2:1; wants accuracy >50% and ideally ~65%. — COVERED
 15. No stop orders (they don't work pre-market anyway); he manages risk manually with hot keys; market orders not allowed pre-market, only limit orders; limit buy = ask + 5-10c for slippage; hotkey sell 30% of position at bid - 10c. — NEW (~62:00-76:00): execution assumption for backtest friction (pay ~5-10c over ask on entry, ~10c under bid on exits on volatile small-caps). Not a signal rule.
 16. Broker/fills: payment-for-order-flow brokers fill slower; for >5,000-share positions use direct-access broker; direct access commission about $2-$5 per trade (negligible at 15-30k shares). — NEW minor (~58:30-60:30). Cost modelling only.
 17. Sizing, starter size: start each day at 1/4 size (full 20,000 sh -> 5,000 sh starter) until up 20 cents per full-size share (= $1,000 cushion); only then full size; if P&L dips back below the $1,000 cushion, drop back to quarter size; never full size on a red day. — NEW (numeric) (~93:00-97:00). Partly overlaps COVERED "size up only after a cushion" but gives the numbers (cushion = $0.20 x full size; starter = 25%).

 20. Quality tiers: A setups accuracy 80-90% hot market, 2:1; ~5-10 A trades/day hot, 1-3 cold; add B then C setups only while profit still rises; stop when diminishing returns; target "give back about 10% of the day's profit" as the marker that you pushed to the edge. — NEW (the ~10% giveback marker; COVERED is the stop-after-half-giveback rule, a different threshold) (~98:30-101:30)

Base hits vs home runs (his own 10-month analysis, >$300k net): top-25 winners $215k vs top-25 losers ($203k) -> net $12k; winners >$4k totalled $319k vs losers >$2k ($361k) = ($42k); winners $0-$4k = $510k vs losers $0-$2k ($156k) = $354k net. Conclusion: capping win size ~$4k and loss size ~$2k would have made ~$42k MORE; swinging for home runs cost ~$4k/month. Then trading base hits: risk $500 to make $750, ~74-75% accuracy, 30 consecutive green days. Prefers high accuracy/lower P:L over holding for big winners. — NEW (~102:00-110:30). Method codeable: bucket our trade P&L by size, test capped-win/capped-loss variants and profit-target exits at ~1.5R rather than runners.
 22. Many trades on big days: 11-49 trades per big green day (35, 35, 27, 14, 11, 15, 49). — NEW stat (~109:00). Supports high trade count, small size per trade.
 23. Hot vs cold market: hot = more A setups, can trade big size, liquidity high; cold = fewer setups and smaller size possible, "ease off the throttle", size down. — COVERED-ish (regime); no numeric definition. Discretionary.
 24. Daily goal/max loss: a garbled line ("$55,000 daily Max loss", likely $5,000 or similar) — number unreliable; do not use. (~94:50)
 25. Doesn't trade Tesla/Nvidia/mega-caps/GameStop-AMC type names for day trading; trades only stocks with news and 5x RVOL. — COVERED

### KzVbXzkoZkA — Ultimate Guide on ADDING to Winners with Scaling (LIVE STREAM) (1:37:30, year not stated; mentions "2017 to May 2019" account history, HKD/Hudi stocks "last year", OCEA archive "a few months ago")

### GXl6IS4fSOE — This Is My #1 Indicator For Trading (Full Training) [live class "Holy Grail technical indicator"] (1:13:46, ~2023: book "eight years ago" = 2015; GFAI day; chart examples from 2018 and 2020)

### afNhgCc-LCw -- Ultimate Guide to Trading a Short Squeeze for Beginner Traders (1:32:58, ~Nov-Dec 2021 inferred: Avis/CAR squeeze, "1M subscribers by end of this year")
Note: this is an excerpt of Warrior Pro chapter 8 (parabolic momentum) as a live class; mostly stock-type description, entries not taught here.
1. Rules

 3. No-news / unexplained spike stocks carry extra exchange-halt risk (T12 "no known reason" halts, then often a ~50% drop on resumption); pattern is much more common for NYSE-listed than Nasdaq-listed; check which exchange before trading a no-news runner (21:00-22:30) -- NEW
 4. Sympathy plays: when the lead stock rolls over, sympathy names "roll even harder"; sympathy names squeeze with no news of their own (15:00-15:32, 19:00, 30:30) -- NEW (sympathy = trade only the leader is COVERED; the "sympathy falls harder when leader rolls" part is NEW)

 6. Day-selection: if there is no good trade by pre-market, that alone signals it may not be a good day to trade at all (4:01); took no trades the previous day because stocks were range-bound/not moving fast (26:32-27:31) -- NEW
 7. "Hard yes or no": if unsure whether it is the one, it is a no (27:31); wants stock "moving quickly", not choppy/range-bound; slow-mover vs fast-mover distinction (SIRI-style slow stock vs RHE parabolic) (27:00, 52:00) -- NEW
 8. Spread: large bid/ask spread keeps him out of a stock (CAR 5-pt spread, a 55x57 spread "one of the most difficult I have seen"); with 10,000 shares a 3-point drop in the bid is $30k (33:30, 38:30) -- NEW

### PjVivCcM1B0 — How to Trade Pre-Market & After Hours (Step-by-Step Guide) (56:58, ~late 2024/2025; mostly broker mechanics)
1. Rules
 1. Sessions: pre 04:00-09:30, RTH 09:30-16:00, after 16:00-20:00 ET (05:00) — not a rule, reference.
 2. Trades 07:00-09:30 pre-market; sits down ~06:30; most retail brokers don't allow trading before 07:00; volume surge at 07:00 (18:19-20:20) — COVERED (07:00 start).
 3. Regime shift: until ~Mar-Apr 2020 he waited for 09:30; his own metrics: 2016-2019 only ~$111k of profit pre-market, rest from 09:30 on; since 2020 profit peaks 07:00-09:00, less 09:00-11:00, "not net profitable" after hours/afternoon (20:58-24:03) — COVERED (time of day); the pre-2020 vs post-2020 shift is context only.
 4. Setup: wait for the first pop (HFT algo reaction to headline), then a brief pause, buy when it pushes higher (micro pullback); do not buy the first spike before it has pulled back, especially in hot market (15:39-17:39) — COVERED.
 5. News knee-jerk reversal risk: HFT keyword algos buy instantly on headline keywords (e.g. "Google partnership"); retail then reads and reverses if headline is junk; example trade went ~$4.50->~$6.50 then back down (13:34-15:39) — COVERED-ish (algo spike); the junk-headline reversal risk is NEW but not quantified.
 6. Move that starts after hours (16:00) gives the 07:00 pop; news scheduled at round times (08:00, 08:30) (12:26-13:00, 19:01) — NEW (round-time news release; "move starts after hours -> volume pop at 07:00"). Weak/anecdotal.
 7. Pre-market vs RTH microstructure: HFT/book orders appear at 09:30 and vanish at 16:00; HFT algos switch off when stock leaves a standard-deviation band, so news squeezes run further pre/post; RTH is more congestion/consolidation (29:22-31:00, 38:14-41:21) — COVERED (HFT withdraw at +/-10%); the 09:30 book "switch on" is NEW as a regime-change point (relevant to backtest: spread/liquidity pre vs post 09:30).
 8.  9. After hours: no stop orders, no market orders pre/post (limit orders only, with offset above ask); no leverage after hours (margin positions auto-liquidated ~15:45-16:00, squeezes sometimes begin 15:30), leverage allowed pre-market (24:46-26:40, 27:25-28:47) — NEW (execution constraints; margin liquidation 15:45 can cause a 15:30-16:00 squeeze).

### vPMyjSCgf58 — I risked $2,500 to make $76,112.42 (52:41, year not stated; December, "9 trading days left in the year")
1. Rules
 1. Max loss/day = daily goal = $5,000 (goal $5k/day = $1M/yr). COVERED (max daily loss = avg daily gain) [40:29]
 2. Entry only if stop gives >=2:1 reward:risk; stop = low of the pullback candle(s); if the support is far away (e.g. 4 green candles back) skip. "No RR = no trade". Max-loss on that trade was -$0.10 (low of pullback), 25,000 sh = $2,500 risk. COVERED (2:1, micro pullback) — stop = pullback low is mostly covered; explicit "skip if pullback low is too far away / after 11 straight green candles with no pullback" is NEW-ish [2:05-3:07]
 3. Do not buy a topping-tail extension even if MACD positive and volume good; require a pullback. COVERED (extended / MACD)
 4. First-trade size cap: trade 1/4 of full size until up $0.20/share cumulative profit (i.e. a cushion); scale to full only after hitting that first profit target; if never reached, stay 1/4 size all day. NEW [31:08]
 5. Three strikes: after 3 consecutive losses at quarter size, stop for the day ("market is cold"). NEW [32:11-32:42]
 6. After a first loss, next trade still quarter size; size back DOWN if equity falls below the initial first-profit-target level. NEW [34:15]
 7. Stop trading after giving back ~10% (10-15%) off the day's peak profit; give back 50% = hard stop. COVERED in part (50% rule covered); 10-15% off the peak is NEW [33:43, 37:21]
 8. Does not stop at the daily goal; keeps trading while hot. NEW (soft; contradicts a hard goal stop) [33:43]
 9. Price band: his March (bad month) metrics showed edge only in $2-$10; April change = no stocks over $20 -> big turn. $2-$10 sweet spot. NEW (narrower than the $2-$20 pillar) [20:10-20:42]
10. Review metrics by time of day (stop afternoons) and by price/volume. COVERED (afternoons)
11. Hot-market regime: after a very hot prior day (a stock +2,000% in 3 days) be aggressive, bigger size (this day started at ~1/3 to 1/2 of full ~75,000 sh), jump in faster; cold market: 1-3 setups, ease off. Also warns a big green day is often followed by a big red day / junk squeezes with no catalyst that roll over. NEW [43:34, 44:36, 50:51]
12. Size is liquidity-limited: full 75,000 sh only on stocks with ~57-100M volume and low price; too-expensive stock (PRFX ~$7-10) gets less. NEW [43:02]
14. On parabolic stocks that do not pull back on 1-min, trades the 10-second chart micro pullbacks. NEW [47:11, 48:15]
15. Quality tiers: A setups ~80-90% accuracy and 2:1 P/L; B and C setups lower accuracy and P/L; add B/C only in a hot market, diminishing returns. NEW (discretionary grading) [35:50-37:21]
16. Breakout level: entry looks for break through the pullback high AND a psychological whole/half-dollar level ($7, then $7.50 next target). NEW [14:30]
17. Breakout or bailout, ~4 min average hold. COVERED
18. Adding: once added full size at $8.00 on a pullback to double up for a break of yesterday's high; lost $3,300; he calls it risky, only done with a large cushion. NEW (add-on with cushion) [45:38-46:08]
19. No catalyst found on a squeezing stock = hesitated/skipped initially. COVERED-ish (news)
2. Stats (self-reported): year to date ~$1.9M, avg daily gain ~$8,000; accuracy ~65%; avg win $877, avg loss $592 (~1.5:1); avg hold 4 min [17:36-18:37]. Losing days accuracy 46% vs green days ~70% [28:01]. 47 consecutive green days after the quarter-size rule [34:48]. Last red day was Nov 30 (-$6,000); Nov gross $339k. Day: +$76,120 (first trade PRFX +$8,300 on $2,500 risk = 3:1; TPET ~+$21k; PBM -$690; NUKK -$3,300; amounts for PRFX/BPTH garbled in captions). Break-even accuracy 50% at 1:1, 33% at 2:1, 25% at 3:1.
3. Codeable?
 - Quarter-size until +$0.20/sh cushion; size back down below it: codeable from 1-min bars (sizing logic in backtest, needs a fills-based running P&L)
 - 3 consecutive losses = stop for day: codeable
 - 10-15% off peak profit stop: codeable (running P&L)
 - $2-$10 price band: codeable from daily/1-min bars
 - Liquidity-limited size (volume 57-100M): codeable from daily bars
 - 10-second pullbacks: needs 10-sec bars (IBKR can give 5-sec; not 1-min only)
 - Whole/half-dollar breakout level: codeable from 1-min bars
 - Hot/cold regime from prior-day leaders: codeable from daily bars (a leader up >X% over 3 days) but threshold undefined
 - Quality A/B/C tiers, adds with cushion: discretionary (no defined criteria)
4. Most useful NEW: the quarter-size-until-+$0.20/share-cushion rule plus the 3-strike stop (the day-start sizing ramp), which he credits with 47 straight green days; testable as a position-sizing/stop overlay on MCL/MC5.
5. Portion read: 100% (203/203 lines, to 52:37)

### hz7vhSIXXSc — Dip Trading was HARD Until I Learned These 3 Simple Tricks (51:55, 2020 turning point; mentions 2022-2023 bear market; year of video not stated)
1. Rules
 1. Dip checklist step 1, volume profile: volume must INCREASE as price moves up (green volume rising); on the pullback, NO high-volume red (selling) candle; light-volume red candles only. Price up with volume declining = red flag/divergence. Higher volume selling than green = stop. COVERED partly ("heavier green volume") — the "no high-volume selling candle on the pullback, 1-4 light red candles" part is NEW [3:07-7:55, 14:16]
 2. Step 2: price at or above the 9 EMA on the 1-min chart; a sustained break below the 9 EMA = no trade (brief flush below then reclaim is acceptable and a good entry). COVERED (9 EMA) in spirit; the "sub-9EMA flush then reclaim = buy" is NEW [17:25, 21:36]
 3. Step 3: MACD "open" (MACD line above signal line); a crossover below = consolidation/no trade; "walk away" when it crosses over after a run. COVERED (MACD positive) — note: here defined as MACD above SIGNAL, not above zero [18:28, 33:03, 38:12]
 4. Step 4 bonus (Level 2): look for green on the tape (buying at the ask) after the dip and no big seller on L2 at the level (e.g. 100,000-share seller at $9). COVERED (L2 unreliable) though he uses it; tape/L2 = NEW but not codeable [19:29-23:09]
 5. Prefer entries at/near whole and half dollars (psychological support), dip to a whole dollar that coincides with the 9 EMA = two supports. NEW [20:33-21:36]
 6. Stop = low of the pullback; starter exit "loss usually under 10 cents/share (5-7 cents)"; accept small stabs if checklist passes. COVERED (stop) / 10c-per-share loss size NEW-ish [15:19, 24:09]
 7. Never full size on a dip entry: starter then add on first candle to make a new high / high-of-day break. Example: 1,000 starter at 8.98 (risk ~$100), add 1,000 at 9.15, add 1,000 at 9.25 -> 3,000 sh, avg ~9.18, exit ~10 for ~$2,000 (20:1). Add only to winners, never to losers. COVERED ("no averaging down"); starter-then-add pyramid structure NEW [25:13-26:47, 29:22-30:25]
 8. Dip trades have lower win rate but higher P/L than breakout trades; breakout trades = confirmation but "you pay a price" with higher entry. NEW (qualitative) [13:13-14:16, 25:13]
 9. Trade only first and second pullback; by the third pullback risk rises ("grinder"). NEW [31:59, 46:05]
10. Do not take dip if it is sideways under the high or does not go through the high (hidden-seller / iceberg, e.g. repeated stall under 13.50). NEW [7:55, 30:55, 31:28]
11. Big high-volume rejection candles = insider dumping; reduce share size in big-range extended area; walk away. NEW [35:07, 37:40]
12. Below VWAP = not the right place to be a buyer (open with high-volume selling, price below VWAP). COVERED (VWAP break)
13. Continuation setups from the previous day work less well ("continuation from previous day typically does not work as well"). NEW [36:08-36:39]
14. Trading window about 07:00-11:00 ET, not after hours. COVERED
15. Obvious stock with catalyst/volume only; no S&P e-mini chop. COVERED
16. Starter size cap: first trades 5,000 sh (~10-20% of full 30-50k) until up $1,000 on the day (20c/share); size down again if profit falls back to $1,000. NEW (variant of the quarter-size rule: here ~10-20% not 25%) [40:48-41:51]
17. After crossing daily goal: trailing stop on the day at 50% of profit (e.g. goal 5,000 -> stop at 2,500; rising with peak); give back 10-20% -> re-apply the 5,000-share cap; give back 50% -> shut down and walk away. COVERED (50%) / 10-20% re-cap NEW [42:22-43:24]
18. After red days, put restrictions (size caps) back on; remove them in a hot market. NEW (regime-linked size caps) [44:29-45:33]
19. Breakout-vs-dip: breakout trade = instant resolution; entries at "break of high of day". COVERED
2. Stats (self-reported): lifetime $12.6M profit, accuracy 68.6%, avg hold ~5 min, P/L now ~1:1 (damaged by a -$240,000 loss in the GameStop period; also a +$140,000 winner) [48:09-48:39]. Early-career: avg win ~12 cents/share vs avg loss ~24 cents/share (1:2, needs 66.7% to break even) [47:07]. Moving from 50% to 60% accuracy by quality focus. Red days: -$5,800 (March). Stretch with >$100k profit with restrictions off.
3. Codeable?
 - Volume profile (rising volume on up-leg, low-volume red pullback candles): codeable from 1-min bars
 - 9 EMA hold with brief flush and reclaim: codeable
 - MACD(12,26,9) line > signal: codeable (note: differs from "MACD>0" in earlier-covered rule — worth testing both)
 - Whole/half-dollar proximity for entry: codeable
 - Starter + add on first new high structure; first-two-pullbacks only (count pullbacks since the move began): codeable
 - Cap size until +$0.20/share (or $1,000) day profit; give-back 10-20% re-cap; 50% trailing day stop: codeable
 - Previous-day continuation = lower quality: codeable from daily bars (stock was a leader yesterday)
 - Iceberg/hidden seller from stall under a level, L2 big seller, tape green: needs level 2 / tape (stall under a level partly codeable from 1-min bars)
 - Insider dump rejection candle: discretionary
4. Most useful NEW: pullback-count and volume-profile gate for dip entries (only 1st/2nd pullback; no high-volume red candle; whole/half-dollar support) — directly testable on MCL/MC5 entries.
5. Portion read: 100% (199/199 lines, to 51:53)

### h53KLB2liR0 — -275K BIGGEST RED DAY EVER (45:04, Feb 4 2021 per context: GameStop period, "February 4th", Thursday)
1. Rules (almost all are post-mortem lessons; there is little rule content)
 1. Loss trigger sequence: gave back a +$27k morning on a false breakout (AACG: add of ~50,000 sh at break of $5, high 4.90, flush) -> immediate "FOMO mode" into next stocks; each subsequent trade bigger. Rule implied: after giving back a large open profit to break-even, stop trading. COVERED (stop after giving back profit) [6:19-7:21, 15:11-15:43]
 2. Do not hold two positions at once (split focus); he "almost never" does. NEW [17:14] (one concurrent position cap)
 3. Do not add to a stock for a break of a level on a thin, no-news "leading gap" with no news; AACG had no news. COVERED (news) / and no averaging down COVERED
 4. Halt resumption trades: buying into a halt resume / hammering buy on a resume produced huge slippage: 42,000 sh filled 29.21 on CPSH, dropped to 24 (~$240k loss, ~5 points on ~44,000 sh); halts down at 25. Lesson: avoid large size across/at halt resumption; avoid holding a second halted stock. NEW [9:27-12:50]
 5. Order-stacking error: cancel/exit orders stacked in the active orders window blocked fills; exit rule used was "stop at VWAP ($24)". Process, not a signal. NEW but operational [11:01-12:33]
 6. Cut losses always; did not hold AVXL down 4 points (15,000 sh = -$60k). COVERED
 7. Account hard limits (max loss) exist on his account but sized for his range; he proposes adding restrictions and taking 1-2 days off (Friday + weekend) after the loss. NEW (cool-off after a very large loss) [42:09-42:41]
 8. After a big red day: no "hail mary" revenge trades, rebuild one small win at a time ("one bucket at a time"); trade smaller. NEW qualitative [33:19-35:22]
 9. Emotional days (birthday, anniversary) have higher stakes; scale back when life circumstances are off. Discretionary.
10. Max risk per day: said he could have capped at 5,000/day but rejected small-size alternative; hot days vs cold days argue against a fixed 5k stop. COVERED-ish.
2. Stats (self-reported): day -$274-275k (biggest ever; roughly 3x his prior worst; CPSH -$240k on ~44,000 sh x ~5 points); prior Monday -$95,000; last week +~$900,000 incl. one +$478k day; YTD before the day ~$800k, after ~$500k; 2020 total ~$5M ("583 to over $5M"); AACG morning +$27k round-tripped to ~-$5k; AVXL -$30k on 15,000 sh at $25; WAFU -$3k; ANVS profit ~$2k [12:33]. 2016-2017 profits combined < the $500k YTD.
3. Codeable?
 - One position at a time / no concurrent positions: codeable (portfolio constraint in backtest)
 - Do not trade through halt resumptions: partly codeable (1-min bar gaps/ halt detection is not reliably in IBKR bars; needs halt/news feed or tape)
 - Cool-off after big loss / daily loss lockout (stop after giving back large open profit to flat): codeable
 - Order-stack/execution errors: not strategy rules
4. Most useful NEW: concurrency cap (one open position) and "no new entries after a morning profit has round-tripped to ~flat" as a lockout, plus no sizing up on halt-resume names; otherwise nothing new on entries.
5. Portion read: 100% (173/173 lines, to 44:57)

### ntM09DJj15E — How Much Risk Should I Take? (25:03, year not stated; "first day of August lost $44,000", a cool market)
1. Rules
 1. Pillars here stated as: price $2-$20 (COVERED); % up today minimum 25% (hot market: 25% not enough) (COVERED, 25% differs from >=10% pillar: NEW threshold) and prefer top-3 % gainer (COVERED); RVOL >=5 (COVERED), "no such thing as too high"; news yes/no (COVERED); float under 20M, lower is better (COVERED, floats <20M variant). [1:34-5:42]
 2. Price sweet spot $5-$10 is where he makes the most money; profit by price low below $5, peak 5-10, tapering above (GameStop an outlier ~1.5% of lifetime profit). NEW (also contradicts "$2-$20 flat") [2:37-3:40]
 3. Edge-of-range stocks (price near $2 or $20, % up barely 25%, RVOL barely 5, float near 20M) = "on the cusp" -> less risk or skip. Low price = grinders (no big breakouts); high price = wider spreads/bigger losses. NEW [6:14-7:15]
 4. Low price + high float = grinder (crowded, large blocks, slow). Same stock with 1.9M float vs 19M changes the decision; $5, up 26%, RVOL 5.5, float 1.9M -> would definitely trade; float 19M -> hesitate. Rule: demand/volume relative to float drives % change (volume/float ratio: 2M float with 20M volume could go +100%; 20M float needs ~200M volume). NEW (volume-to-float turnover) [7:46-9:53]
 5. Risk scaling: take MORE risk (size) with higher RVOL, higher % gain, lower float, price in $5-$10, hot market; example stock $5, +75%, RVOL 20, float 1.9M gets more risk even in a cold market. NEW (size = f(pillar strength)) [10:24-10:55]
 6. Market regime float cutoff: hot market trades floats up to ~20M (more trades); cold market cut off at about 5M float and trades fewer; only low floats can squeeze when demand is thin. NEW [11:28-12:59]
 7. Skip stocks with a fixed-value buyout news ("price fixed at $5"; WOW example). NEW (news type exclusion: acquisition at fixed price) [14:00-14:30]
 8. Skip: price <$2 (XFOR just under $2, 5M float, RVOL 159, news yes -> skipped "too cheap, no potential for another 50%"); low RVOL + light total volume + small % gain (ZENA +12%, VVPR +5%) -> instant no-go; float >100M below $2 (JVO 200M) no. COVERED mostly; price <$2 reason and tiny-% skip COVERED
 9. Chart skip: stock moved after hours/prior-day tricky history, below premarket high and under resistance (5.75) -> not trusted even though selection passes. Pullback "grindy" (very high volume but small rate of change) after six green candles -> skipped. Do not trade the 09:30 open candle (doji first candle). Do not buy into a double top / right into resistance (no adds into 5.75). Level 2 "very thick, crowded" -> skip. NEW (grindy = volume high but price change small; no trades at open first candle; 6 consecutive green then pullback = skip) [17:08-21:18]
10. MACD cross negative = sustained pullback -> no entry. COVERED
11. Regime / risk reduction: after a -$44,000 day (Aug 1) he traded small Mon-Wed, then two NO-TRADE days when the market was cool and he was back at all-time highs ("foot off the gas"); lean in only if something is really good. Reduced account size/buying power as a self-imposed cap. NEW (cold-market = trade only exceptional setups; no-trade days) [21:49-23:53]
2. Stats (self-reported): lifetime profit by price peaks at $5-$10; GameStop ~1.5% of lifetime profit; a -$44,000 day on Aug 1; ~+$10-12k on the month at the time; today's leader ATNF up ~72%, volume 68M, RVOL 35; others RVOL 5,000 (RMBL), 500. No win-rate or P/L numbers.
3. Codeable?
 - Price sweet-spot $5-$10 and edge-of-range scoring (price near 2/20; % near 25; RVOL near 5; float near 20M): codeable from daily + 1-min bars (float/news needs float data)
 - Volume-to-float turnover (day volume / float): needs float data (daily bars for volume)
 - Cold market cap float <= 5M vs hot <= 20M: needs float data + a regime proxy (e.g. count of stocks up >25% with RVOL>5, or leader's % gain)
 - Size scaling by pillar strength: needs float / news data; RVOL/% gain/price codeable
 - Fixed-price buyout skip: needs news data
 - "Grindy" (high volume per bar, small range) filter: codeable from 1-min bars (volume vs range ratio)
 - No entry on first 1-min candle at the open: codeable
 - No-trade days when market cold: partly codeable via regime proxy
4. Most useful NEW: float-tier by market regime plus volume/float turnover as the "squeeze potential" measure, and the $5-$10 price sweet spot as a size/score weight (testable on existing trade logs by price bucket and float bucket).
5. Portion read: 100% (97/97 lines, to 25:00)
